      minhash: 4mvXROGO0ZklLP9szLvm2XBYDtJKIcYtVjjJQBrO5RaDU/WpXCHW7a8WRBh8s3RSLs9kGjtztqdTOKgJFHDmhbAY47M5BFLkH+ZKHJsPlHC+wEoYG7ZjQ3dy8exa3TjboPH1kBkelO6JVWO49TFhDff6DYJDc6SUnBdiGTVi1Rbz+7818AhOfDgG11wq3knQnrx1JfytzmFhOf/uEuj+wlABCx12uSYgtiNeK9skGNoVyHbnLoaicRCuluGifk5TowdlqAiaIY5GX22rFXNHU7LuVK1c/Lgc5CL5Z3h0GRL+kuMFtYB7BNEDCITvt8Dq1yhKWkwvd+82Q1Lc7jsGxg==

<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
Let $d\geq 1$, $1\leq\beta\leq 2$, $k\geq 2$ and $\mu \in \{-1,1\}$. Then \label{Local_theory:lwp_main}
    \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} is locally well-posed in \(H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)\) for
    \begin{equation*}
        \begin{cases}
            s > \tfrac{d}{2}, & \beta = 1\\
            s \geq \max\left(d\left(\tfrac{1}{2} - \tfrac{1}{k}\right), \tfrac{d}{2} - \tfrac{\beta-1}{k-1}\right), & \beta > 1
        \end{cases}
    \end{equation*}
    with existence time \(T \sim \|u_0\|_{H^s}^{1-k}\).
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- question-1: body_md -->
Let $d,\mu =1$. For the following values of $s$, $k$ and $\beta$
\begin{itemize}
    \item \underline{Case 1:} $1\leq \beta \leq 1 + \tfrac{k-1}{2k}$ and $\tfrac{1}{2} - \tfrac{\beta -1}{k-1} \leq s \leq \tfrac{1}{2}$,
    \item \underline{Case 2:} $\tfrac{3}{2}-\tfrac{1}{2k} < \beta \leq 2-\tfrac{1}{k}$ and $\tfrac{1}{2} - \tfrac{\beta -1}{k-1} \leq s < \tfrac{k-1}{2k}$,
    \item \underline{Case 3:} $2- \tfrac{1}{k} < \beta \leq 2$ and $\tfrac{1}{2} - \tfrac{\beta -1}{k-1} \leq s < \tfrac{1}{2} - \tfrac{1}{k}$,
\end{itemize}
is \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} well or ill-posed in $H^s$?
<!-- end question-1 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{Global:thm}
    Let $d=1$, $\mu \in \{-1,1\}$, $k\geq 3$ be an odd integer and $\tfrac{k-2}{k+1} +1 < \beta \leq 2$ be a real number. Then, \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} is globally well-posed in $H^s(\mathbb{T})$ in the following regimes:
    \begin{itemize}
        \item \underline{Regime 1:} $2-\tfrac{2}{k+1} < \beta \leq 2$ and $\tfrac{1}{2} - \tfrac{1}{k+1} \leq s < \tfrac{\beta}{2}$
        \item \underline{Regime 2:} $k=3$, $\tfrac{5}{4} <\beta \leq \tfrac{3}{2}$ and $\tfrac{9}{8} - \tfrac{\beta}{2}< s <\tfrac{\beta}{2}$
        \item \underline{Regime 3:} $\tfrac{2k-2}{k+1} \leq \beta \leq 2- \tfrac{2}{k+1}$ and $s > \tfrac{1}{2}$.
    \end{itemize}
    Furthermore, we obtain a polynomial growth bound in time on the $H^s$ norm of the solution $u(t)$ for Regimes 1 and 2 and a super exponential bound in time for Regime 3.
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{thm:global:endpoint}
    Let $\mu \in \{-1,1\}$. Then \eqref{Intro:cRBO} is globally well-posed in $H^{1/2}(\mathbb{T})$.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{THM:dim_2}
    \eqref{dim_2:cubic_BBM} is globally wellposed in $H^s$ for $s\geq \tfrac{1}{2}$. Moreover, we obtain an exponential growth bound on the $H^s$ norm of the solution for $\tfrac{1}{2} \leq s <2$.
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{Intersection:Lk1:local:thm}
    Let, $d=1$, $\mu \in \{-1,1\}$, $k\geq 3$ be odd and,
    \begin{align*}
        1+ \tfrac{k-1}{k+1} \leq \beta \leq 2.
    \end{align*} 
    Then \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} is globally well-posed in $L^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})$.
    
    Furthermore, if $d=2$, $\mu \in \{-1,1\}$, $k=3$ and $\beta=2$, then \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} is globally well-posed in $L^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}^2)$.
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
\label{cor:almost_sure_global}
    Let $d=1$, $\mu \in \{-1,1\}$, $k\geq 3$ be odd and,
    \begin{align*}
        1+ \tfrac{k-1}{k+1} \leq \beta \leq 2.
    \end{align*}
    Then \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} is almost-surely globally well-posed in $H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)$ for all $s \geq 0$.
    
    Furthermore, if $d=2, \mu \in \{-1,1\}$, $k=3$ and $\beta=2$, \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} is almost surely globally well-posed in $H^s(\mathbb{T}^2)$ for all $s\geq 0$.
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{InfiniteLossofRegs}
    Let $k\geq 2 $, $1\leq \beta\leq 2$, $\mu \in \{-1,1\}$, $s<0$ and $d\geq 1$. Then \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} is ill-posed in $H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)$. In particular, it exhibits an infinite loss of regularity. That is, for $\sigma \in \mathbb{R}$, $s<0$, $u_0\in H^s(\mathbb{T})$ and any $\varepsilon>0$, there exists a smooth solution $u_\varepsilon$ to \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} and a time $t_\varepsilon = (0,\varepsilon)$ such that,
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|u_\varepsilon(0)-u_0\right\|_{H^s} < \varepsilon \quad \text{and} \quad \left\|u_\varepsilon(t_\varepsilon)\right\|_{H^{\sigma}} > \varepsilon^{-1}.
    \end{align*}
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{lem:Brezis-Gallouet}
    For $u \in H^s(\mathbb{T})$ with $s>1/2$, there exists a constant $C>0$ such that,
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|u\right\|_{L^\infty} \leq C\left(1+\sqrt{\log\left(1+\left\|u\right\|_{H^s}\right)}\left\|u\right\|_{H^{1/2}}\right).
    \end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{Lem:Trudinger_type}
    There exists a constant $C>0$ such that for all $2<p<\infty$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|u\right\|_{L^p(\mathbb{T})}\leq C \sqrt{p}\left\|u\right\|_{H^{1/2}(\mathbb{T})}.
    \end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{Interpolation:Brezis:THM}
     Let $0<\theta<1$, $1\leq p, p_0,p_1\leq \infty$, $s,s_1,s_2\in \mathbb{R}$ and $d\in \mathbb{N}$ so that,
    \begin{align*}
        s &= \theta s_2 + (1-\theta)s_2, &
        \frac{1}{p} &= \frac{\theta}{p_1} + \frac{1-\theta}{p_2}\label{Preliminaries:interp:cond}.
    \end{align*}
    Then we have the following,
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|u\right\|_{W^{s,p}(\mathbb{T}^d)} \lesssim \left\|u\right\|_{W^{s_1,p_1}(\mathbb{T})}^{\theta} \left\|u\right\|^{1-\theta}_{W^{s_2,p_2}(\mathbb{T}^d)}.
    \end{align*}
<!-- end theorem-7 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{Interpolation:GNS:THM}
    Fix \(u\in W^{s_1,p_1}(\mathbb{T}^d)\cap W^{s_2,p_2}(\mathbb{T}^d)\). Assume that the real numbers \(0\leq s_1,s_2,\delta\), \(\theta\in (0,1)\) and \(1\leq p_1,p_2,p \leq \infty\) are such that the following is satisfied,
    \begin{align*}
        \theta\left(s_1-\frac{d}{p_1}\right) + (1-\theta) \left(s_2 - \frac{d}{p_2}\right) & \geq \delta -\frac{d}{p} \\
        \theta s_1 + (1-\theta)s_2                                                                             \geq \delta.
    \end{align*}
    Then the following holds,
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\|u\right\|_{W^{\delta,p}(\mathbb{T}^d)} \lesssim \left\|u\right\|^\theta_{W^{s_1,p_1}(\mathbb{T}^d)} \left\|u\right\|^{1-\theta}_{W^{s_2,p_2}(\mathbb{T}^d)}.
    \end{equation*}
    Furthermore for the case \(W^{s_1,p_1}(\mathbb{T}^d) = H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)\) and \(W^{s_2,p_2}(\mathbb{T}^d) = L^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}^d)\) we have that,
    \begin{equation*}
        \theta = \frac{\delta + \frac{d}{k+1} -\frac{d}{p}}{s-\frac{d}{2} + \frac{d}{k+1}}.
    \end{equation*}
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
This follows from the Sobolev inequality and Theorem \ref{Interpolation:Brezis:THM}.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{Lem:fractional_Liebniz_rule}
    Let \(1<p_j,q_j \leq \infty\), \(j=1,2\), \(1\leq r <  \infty\) and $s> 0$ such that \(\frac{1}{r} = \frac{1}{p_j} + \frac{1}{q_j}\). Then we have that,
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|uv\right\|_{W^{s,r}} \lesssim \left\|u\right\|_{W^{s,p_1}}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{q_1}} + \left\|u\right\|_{L^{p_1}}\left\|v\right\|_{W^{s,q_2}}.
    \end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
For $s\in \mathbb{R}$ and $1\leq \beta\leq 2$, we define the spaces $H^s_\beta(\mathbb{T})$ by the completion of $C^\infty(\mathbb{T})$ under the norm,
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|u\right\|_{H^s_\beta} := \left\|\langle \partial_x\rangle^{s}_{\beta} u\right\|_{L^2}
    \end{align*}
    where $\langle \partial_x \rangle_\beta$ is a Fourier multiplier with symbol $\langle n\rangle_\beta := \left(1+|n|^\beta\right)^{\frac{1}{\beta}}$.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- lemma-4: body_md -->
\label{Lem:multiplier_lemma}
    For a function $u$ sufficiently regular and integrable, $0<\theta \leq 1$, $1< \beta \leq 2$ and $1<p<\infty$ we have that,
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|\langle \partial_x\rangle^\theta_{\beta} u\right\|_{L^p}\sim \left\|\langle \partial_x\rangle^{\theta} u\right\|_{L^p} \quad \text{and} \quad   \left\|\partial_x u\right\|_{L^p} \lesssim \left\| \langle \partial_x\rangle_\beta u\right\|_{L^p}.\quad 
    \end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-4 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
This result follows by extending the relevent multilpiers to $\mathbb{R}$, using the Mikhlin-H\"ormander Theorem  (see {\cite[Theorem 6.2.7]{grafakos2014a}}) and then transferrence (see \cite[Theorem 4.3.7]{grafakos2014a}) to obtain the desired boundedness over $\mathbb{T}^d$.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- lemma-5: body_md -->
\label{lem:multilinear_estimate}
    For \(1\leq \beta\leq 2\), $d\geq 1$ and \(k \ge 2\), with \(\phi_\beta(D_x)\) defined as the Fourier multiplier with symbol $ \phi(n) = \tfrac{n}{1+|n|^\beta}$. For $u_1,\dots,u_k\in H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)$ we have the following product estimate:
    \begin{equation*}
        \Big\|\phi_\beta(D_x) \prod_{i=1}^k u_i\Big\|_{H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)} \lesssim \prod_{i=1}^k\left\|u_i\right\|_{H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)}
    \end{equation*}
    provided:
    \begin{equation}
        \begin{cases}
            s > \frac{d}{2}, & \beta = 1\\
            s \geq \max\left(d\left(\frac{1}{2} - \frac{1}{k}\right), \frac{d}{2} - \frac{\beta-1}{k-1}\right), & \beta > 1.
        \end{cases}\label{Local:multilinear:s_cond}
    \end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-5 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
For the sake of readability, we prove the case when $u_1=\dots = u_k$, the general case follows in an identical fashion. Inspecting the Fourier multiplier \(\phi_\beta(D_x)\), we note that
    \begin{equation*}
        \widehat{\phi_\beta(D_x)u(n)} = \tfrac{n}{1+|n|^\beta} \widehat{u}(n) \lesssim |n|^{1-\beta} \widehat{u}(n).
    \end{equation*}
    Thus,
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\|{\phi_\beta(D_x) u^k}\right\|_{H^s} = \Big\|{\frac{n}{1+|n|^\beta} \widehat{u^k}(n)}\Big\|_{\ell^2_n} \lesssim \left\|u^k\right\|_{H^{s-\beta+1}}.
    \end{equation*}
    Here, we witness \(\phi_\beta(D_x)\) smoothing an input function by \(\beta-1\) derivatives. As such, it suffices to identify a suitable condition for
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\|u^k\right\|_{H^{s-\beta+1}} \lesssim \left\|u\right\|_{H^s}^k.
    \end{equation*}
    If \(\beta=1\), we observe no smoothing from \(\phi_\beta(D_x)\). This reduces to
    \begin{equation*}
        \left\|u^k\right\|_{H^s} \leq \left\|u\right\|_{H^s}^k.
    \end{equation*}
    This is the algebra property, implying \(H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)\) is a Banach algebra, which is sharply true for \(s > \frac{d}{2}\). Now, assuming that \(\beta>1\) we distinguish two possible cases:

    \underline{Case 1:} Assume that $s-\beta+1 \leq 0$. Here we invoke duality to yield,
    \begin{align}
        \left\|u^k\right\|_{H^{s-\beta+1}}  = \sup_{\left\|g\right\|_{H^{\beta-s-1}} \leq 1} \left|\int_\mathbb{T} u^k g \, dx\right| \leq \sup_{\left\|g\right\| \le 1} \left\|u^k g\right\|_{L^1} \label{Local:multilinear:Minkoski}.
    \end{align}
    Now, using Hölder's inequality:
    \begin{equation*}
        \eqref{Local:multilinear:Minkoski} \le \sup_{\left\|g\right\| \leq 1} \left\|u^k\right\|_{L^{\frac{p}{k}}} \left\|g\right\|_{L^q} = \sup_{\left\|g\right\| \leq 1}\left\|u\right\|_{L^{p}}^k \left\|g\right\|_{L^q}
    \end{equation*}
    where for \(L^{p/k}\) to be a well-defined Banach space we require that \(p\geq k\) and to satisfy Hölder conjugacy we enforce the condition:
    \begin{equation} \label{Local:multilinear:holder_conjugacy}
        \tfrac{k}{p} + \tfrac{1}{q} = 1.
    \end{equation}
    We now seek to verify that we have the following embeddings
    \begin{equation*}
        H^s(\mathbb{T}^d) \hookrightarrow L^{p}(\mathbb{T}^d) \quad \text{and} \quad H^{\beta-s-1}(\mathbb{T}^d) \hookrightarrow L^q(\mathbb{T}^d)
    \end{equation*}
    which would yield the desired estimate like so,
    \begin{align}
        \sup_{\left\|g\right\|_{H^{\beta-s-1}}\leq 1}\left\|u^k g\right\|_{L^1} & \lesssim \sup_{\left\|g\right\|_{H^{\beta-s-1}}\leq 1}\left\|u\right\|^k_{H^s}\left\|g\right\|_{H^{\beta-s-1}} \leq\left\|u\right\|^k_{H^s}\label{Local:multilinear:final_est}.
    \end{align}
    To do this, we use the Sobolev inequality to obtain the condition on \(p\) and \(q\) as such,
    \begin{align*}
        \tfrac{d}{p} \geq \tfrac{d}{2} - s \quad \text{and} \quad \tfrac{d}{q} \geq \tfrac{d}{2} - \beta +s+1.
    \end{align*}
    Simplifying these we get that,
    \begin{align}
        \tfrac{1}{p} \geq \tfrac{1}{2} - \tfrac{s}{d}, \quad \text{and} \quad \tfrac{1}{q} \geq \tfrac{1}{2} + \tfrac{s+1-\beta}{d}\label{Local:multilinear:embedding_cond}.
    \end{align}
    Inspecting the first inequality and using \(p\geq k\) we yield the condition
    \begin{align*}
        \tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{s}{d} \leq \tfrac{1}{k} \, \implies \, s\geq d\left(\tfrac{1}{2} - \tfrac{1}{k}\right)
    \end{align*}
    which is satisfied by assumption \eqref{Local:multilinear:s_cond}.
    Now, verifying the validity of our second embedding as well as our H\"older condition, we combine \eqref{Local:multilinear:embedding_cond} with  \eqref{Local:multilinear:holder_conjugacy} to get
    \begin{align*}
        1\geq k\left(\tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{s}{d}\right) + \tfrac{1}{2} + \tfrac{s+1-\beta}{d} \iff s\geq \tfrac{d}{2} - \tfrac{\beta-1}{k-1}
    \end{align*}
    which is again satisfied by assumption \eqref{Local:multilinear:s_cond}. As such, \eqref{Local:multilinear:final_est} is verified.

    \underline{Case 2:} Now assume that $s-\beta + 1 >0$. Here, we use the fractional Leibniz rule to obtain,
    \begin{align}
        \left\|u^k\right\|_{H^{s-\beta+1}}\lesssim \left\|u\right\|_{W^{s-\beta+1,p}}\left\|u\right\|^{k-1}_{L^{(k-1)q}}\label{Local:multilinear:case_2:fractional_Liebniz}
    \end{align}
    where we have the H\"older condition,
    \begin{align}
        \tfrac{1}{2}=\tfrac{1}{p} + \tfrac{1}{q}\label{Local:multilinear:case_2:Holder_cond}.
    \end{align}
    It remains to verify the following embeddings,
    \begin{align*}
        H^s(\mathbb{T}^d) \hookrightarrow W^{s-\beta+1,p}(\mathbb{T}^d) \quad \text{and} \quad H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)\hookrightarrow L^{(k-1)q}(\mathbb{T}^d)
    \end{align*}
    as with them, we yield the desired estimate upon returning to \eqref{Local:multilinear:case_2:fractional_Liebniz},
    \begin{align}
        \left\|u\right\|_{W^{s-\beta+1,p}(\mathbb{T})}\left\|u\right\|^{k-1}_{L^{(k-1)q}} \lesssim \left\|u\right\|^k_{H^s}.\label{Local:multilinear:case_2:final_ineq}
    \end{align}
    Using the Sobolev inequality we get that,
    \begin{align*}
        s-\tfrac{d}{2} \geq s+1-\beta - \tfrac{d}{p} \iff \tfrac{1}{p} \geq  \tfrac{2-2\beta +d}{2d}
    \end{align*}
    and
    \begin{align*}
        \tfrac{d}{(k-1)q} \geq \tfrac{d}{2} -s \iff \tfrac{1}{q} \geq \tfrac{k-1}{d}\left(\tfrac{1}{2} - s \right).
    \end{align*}
    Given that $q\geq 2$ we require,
    \begin{align}
        \tfrac{1}{2} \geq \tfrac{k-1}{d}\left(\tfrac{1}{2} -s\right) \iff s\geq d\left(\tfrac{1}{2} - \tfrac{1}{2(k-1)}\right).\label{Local:multilinear:q_extra}
    \end{align}
    Combining our lower bounds on $1/p$ and $1/q$ in \eqref{Local:multilinear:case_2:Holder_cond} we get that,
    \begin{align*}
        \tfrac{1}{2} \geq \tfrac{2-2\beta+d}{2d} + \tfrac{k-1}{d}\left(\tfrac{d}{2} -s\right) \iff s\geq \tfrac{2-2\beta +d(k-1)}{2(k-1)}
    \end{align*}
    which is satisfied by assumption \eqref{Local:multilinear:s_cond}. It now remains to verify that \eqref{Local:multilinear:q_extra} is implied by our assumptions. Using our case assumption, $s-\beta+1>0$ in \eqref{Local:multilinear:s_cond} we get that,
    \begin{align*}
        s> \tfrac{2-2(s+1) +d(k-1)}{2(k-1)} \iff s > \tfrac{d(k-1)}{2k}.
    \end{align*}
    A quick calculation verifies that,
    \begin{align*}
        \tfrac{d(k-1)}{2k} >d\left(\tfrac{1}{2} - \tfrac{1}{2(k-1)}\right).
    \end{align*}
    Therefore, \eqref{Local:multilinear:q_extra} is satisfed, yielding our desired estimate, \eqref{Local:multilinear:case_2:final_ineq}.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
This follows simply from Lemma \ref{lem:multilinear_estimate}, the unitarity of $S_\beta(t)$ in $H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)$ and a standard fixed point argument.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
\label{Local:remark_verification}
    To make sense of the regularity condition \eqref{Local:multilinear:s_cond} we note that in dimension 1, the condition
    \begin{align*}
        s \geq \max\left(\tfrac{1}{2} - \tfrac{1}{k}, \tfrac{1}{2} - \tfrac{\beta-1}{k-1}\right)
    \end{align*}
    reduces to the two respective terms in the following cases
    \begin{align*}
        \beta \geq 1+\tfrac{k-1}{k} \implies s\geq \tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{1}{k} \geq  \tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{\beta-1}{k-1}
    \end{align*}
    and,
    \begin{align*}
        \beta < 1+\tfrac{k-1}{k} \implies s\geq \tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{\beta-1}{k-1} > \tfrac{1}{2}-\tfrac{1}{k}.
    \end{align*}
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- lemma-6: body_md -->
\label{Global:lemma:Conservervation}
    For $k\geq 2$, $d\geq 1$, $\mu \in \{-1,1\}$ and $1\leq \beta \leq 2$ the quantities \eqref{Conservation:mass}, \eqref{Conservation:H1_like} and \eqref{Conservation:Hamiltonian} are conserved under the flow of \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq}.
<!-- end lemma-6 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
The conservation of \eqref{Conservation:mass} follows by integrating \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} over $\mathbb{T}^d$, integrating by parts and applying the Lebesgue dominated convergence theorem. The conservation of \eqref{Conservation:H1_like} follows from multiplying \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} through by the solution \(u\), integrating by parts and using Lebesgue dominated convergence. For the conservation of the Hamiltonian, \eqref{Conservation:Hamiltonian}, we first set $d=1$ and rewrite \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} as,
    \begin{align*}
        u_t = -\left(1+|D_x|^\beta\right)^{-1}\partial_{x}\left(u+\mu\tfrac{1}{k}u^k\right).
    \end{align*}
    Now, multiplying both sides by $u+ \frac{\mu}{k} u^k$ and integrating over $\mathbb{T}$ we obtain,
    \begin{align}
        \int_{\mathbb{T}} \left(u+\mu\frac{1}{k} u^k\right)u_t\, dx = -\int_{\mathbb{T}} \left(u+\mu\frac{1}{k}u^k\right)\left(1+|D_x|^\beta\right)^{-1} \partial_{x}\left(u+\mu\frac{1}{k}u^k\right)\, dx.\label{Conservation:Hamiltonian_energy_setup}
    \end{align}
    Applying the chain rule to the term on the left hand side we see,
    \begin{align}
        \int_{\mathbb{T}} \left(u+\mu\frac{1}{k} u^k\right)u_t\, dx &= \int_\mathbb{T} \frac{\partial}{\partial t}\left(\frac{1}{2}u^2 + \mu \frac{1}{k(k+1)}u^{k+1}\right) \, dx \notag\\
        &= \frac{d}{dt} \int_\mathbb{T}\frac{1}{2}u^2 + \mu \frac{1}{k(k+1)}u^{k+1} \, dx\label{Conservation:Hamiltonian:LHS}.
    \end{align}
    Now, inspecting the right hand side of \eqref{Conservation:Hamiltonian_energy_setup} we note that the differential operator $L$ defined,
    \begin{align*}
        L = (1+|D_x|^\beta)^{-1} \partial_{x}
    \end{align*}
    is skew-symmetric. Therefore, if we set $v = u+\mu\frac{1}{k}u^k$,
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{\mathbb{T}} \left(u+\mu\frac{1}{k}u^k\right)\left(1+|D_x|^\beta\right)^{-1} \partial_{x}\left(u+\mu\frac{1}{k}u^k\right)\, dx = \int_{\mathbb{T}} v L v \, dx = 0.
    \end{align*}
    Returning this and \eqref{Conservation:Hamiltonian:LHS} to \eqref{Conservation:Hamiltonian_energy_setup} we obtain \eqref{Conservation:Hamiltonian}. This argument can easily be expanded to the higher dimensions by expanding the sum of the $\partial_{x_i}$ operators and defining,
    \begin{align*}
        L_i = (1+|D_x|^\beta)^{-1} \partial_{x_{i}}
    \end{align*}
    which are also skew-symmetric for each $1\leq i\leq d$.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{Global:supercritical:Global}
    For $k\geq 2$, $1< \beta\leq 2$, $\mu \in \{-1,1\}$ and $d \geq 1$ or  $k=3$, $\beta=1$, $\mu \in \{-1,1\}$ and $d=1$,   \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} is Globally well posed in \(H^s(\mathbb{T}^d)\) for \(s\geq \beta/2\).
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
By the above logic, if  \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} is locally well posed in $H^{\beta/2}$ then by a standard iteraction argument, there exists a global solution. Now, we need only to verify the conditions of Theorem \ref{Local_theory:lwp_main}. For $\beta >1$, Remark \ref{Local:remark_verification} will help us in this process as we need to check only two cases for different ranges of $\beta$ and $k$.
    \begin{itemize}
        \item For $\beta \geq 1+\frac{k-1}{k}$ we require that $\frac{\beta}{2} \geq \frac{1}{2} - \frac{1}{k}$. This is clearly satisfied by the positivity of $k$ and the fact that $\beta\geq 1$.
        \item For $\beta < 1+\frac{k-1}{k}$ we require that $\frac{\beta}{2} \geq \frac{1}{2} - \frac{\beta-1}{k-1}$, which is again satisfied by the fact that $\beta\geq 1$ and that $k\geq 2$.
    \end{itemize}
    For the case of $k=3$, $\beta=1$, $\mu \in \{-1,1\}$ and $d=1$  we must use our local well-posedness result from Theorem \ref{THM:Local:endpoint} in $H^{1/2}(\mathbb{T})$.

    Having checked these cases, we can say that a local solution exists and resultantly a global one. The result for $s>\beta/2$ follows by  the standard argument.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- lemma-7: body_md -->
\label{global:critical:general:lem_energy}
    For a smooth solution \(u\) to \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} with data $u_0$ and $d\geq 1$ we have the following,
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt} \left\|u \right\|^2_{H^s_\beta(\mathbb{T}^d)} =  -\frac{\mu}{k}\left\langle \langle D_x\rangle^{2s-\beta}_\beta u,\sum_{i=1}^d \partial_{x_i} \left((u)^k\right) \right\rangle_{L^2(\mathbb{T}^d)}
    \end{align*}
    where for $d=1$ the sum on the left hand is simply the first partial derivative in space, $\partial_x$.
<!-- end lemma-7 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
We multiply \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} through by \(\left(1+|D_x|^{\beta}\right)^{\frac{2s-\beta}{\beta}}u\) to obtain,
    \begin{align*}
        \left(1+|D_x|^{\beta}\right)^{\frac{2s-\beta}{\beta}}u \partial_t(1+|D_x|^\beta) u + \left(1+ |D_x|^\beta\right)^{\frac{2s-\beta}{\beta}} u \sum_{i=1}^d \partial_{x_i}\left(u + \frac{\mu}{k}u^k\right) = 0.
    \end{align*}
    Integrating over \(\mathbb{T}\), we observe that the term containing $\partial_{x_i} u$ is zero for all $1\leq i \leq d$. This yields, 
    \begin{align}
        \int_{\mathbb{T}^d} \left(1+|D_x|^{\beta}\right)^{\frac{2s-\beta}{\beta}}u \partial_t(1+|D_x|^\beta) u \, dx = -\int_{\mathbb{T}^d}\frac{\mu}{k}\left(1+|D_x|^{\beta}\right)^{\frac{2s-\beta}{\beta}}u \sum_{i=1}^d \partial_{x_i} \left(u^k\right) \, dx\label{global:critical:general:strict_energy_equality}.
    \end{align}
    Using the self-adjointness of our Fourier multipliers we obtain,
    \begin{align}
        \left\langle \left(1+|D_x|^\beta\right)^{\frac{2s-\beta}{\beta}} u, \partial_t\left(1+|D_x|^\beta\right)u \right\rangle_{L^2} = \left\langle \langle D_x\rangle^{s}_\beta u, \partial_t \langle D_x\rangle^{s}_\beta u \right\rangle_{L^2}= \frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt} \left\|u\right\|_{H^s_\beta}\label{Global:General:critical:Energy_identity:LHS_lower_bound}.
    \end{align}
    Now writing the right hand side of \eqref{global:critical:general:strict_energy_equality} as an inner product,
    \begin{align*}
        -\frac{\mu}{k}\int_{\mathbb{T}^d} \left(1+|D_x|\right)^{\frac{2s-\beta}{\beta}} u\sum_{i=1}^d \partial_{x_i} \left(u^k\right)\, dx = -\frac{\mu}{k}\left\langle \langle D_x\rangle^{2s-\beta}_\beta u,\sum_{i=1}^d \partial_{x_i} \left(u^k\right) \right\rangle_{L^2}.
    \end{align*}
    Combining the above with \eqref{Global:General:critical:Energy_identity:LHS_lower_bound} in \eqref{global:critical:general:strict_energy_equality} we get the stated result.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-8: body_md -->
\label{Global:critical:general:Lk1_control}
    For \(k\) odd and \(1\leq \beta \leq 2\), a solution $u$ to \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} with initial data $u_0\in L^{k+1}(\mathbb{T}^d)$  we have that for $t>0$,
    \begin{equation}
        \left\|u(t)\right\|_{L^{k+1}} \lesssim_{k,\mu,d}  \mathscr{H}_{k,1,d}(u_0)\label{Global:critical:general:Lk1_ineq}
    \end{equation}
    where $\mathscr{H}_{k,1,d}$ is the Hamiltonain of \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} defined \eqref{Conservation:Hamiltonian}.
<!-- end lemma-8 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
If \(\mu =1\), our Hamiltonian conservation, \eqref{Conservation:Hamiltonian} gives us that,
    \begin{align*}
        \mathscr{H}_{k,1,d}(u(t)) = \int_{\mathbb{T}^d} \frac{1}{2}u(t)^2 + \frac{1}{k(k+1)}u(t)^{k+1}\, dx = \int_{\mathbb{T}^d} \frac{1}{2}u_0^2 + \frac{1}{k(k+1)}u_0^{k+1}\, dx = \mathscr{H}_{k,1,d}(u_0)
    \end{align*}
    for all $t \in [0,T]$.
    Given \(k\) is odd, $u(t)^{k+1}\geq 0$ for all $x\in \mathbb{T}^d$. As such, we may use the definition of the \(L^{k+1}\) norm to obtain \eqref{Global:critical:general:Lk1_ineq}.

    Now we consider the \(\mu =-1\) case. Multiplying \eqref{Conservation:Hamiltonian} by \(-1\) we get that,
    \begin{align}
        \int_{\mathbb{T}^d} \frac{1}{k(k+1)}u(t)^{k+1} \, dx -\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{2}u(t)^2\, dx = \int_{\mathbb{T}^d} \frac{1}{k(k+1)}u_0^{k+1} \, dx -\int_{\mathbb{T}}\frac{1}{2}u_0^2\, dx\label{u_Lham_bounded:mu_minus:ident}
    \end{align}
    Now, using Young's inequality on our $u(t)^2$ integral term we obtain,
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{T}^d} u(t)^2 \, dx \leq C_\varepsilon |\mathbb{T}^d| + \varepsilon \int_{\mathbb{T}^d} u^{k+1} \, dx
    \end{align*}
    for some \(\varepsilon >0\) and \(C_\varepsilon>0\) depending on \(\varepsilon\). Using this in \eqref{u_Lham_bounded:mu_minus:ident} we get that,
    \begin{align*}
        \left(\frac{1}{k(k+1)} - \varepsilon\right) \int_{\mathbb{T}^d}u^{k+1}\, dx \leq \int_{\mathbb{T}^d} \frac{1}{2}u_0^2 + \frac{1}{k(k+1)}u_0^{k+1}\, dx + C_\varepsilon|\mathbb{T}^d|.
    \end{align*}
    Setting \(\varepsilon < \frac{1}{k(k+1)}\) and rearanging, we obtain \eqref{Global:critical:general:Lk1_ineq}.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\label{Regime_1_THM}
    Let $d =1$, $\mu \in \{-1,1\}$, $k\geq 3$ be an odd integer and $2-\frac{2}{k+1} \leq \beta \leq 2$. Then \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} is globally  wellposed in $H^s$ for,
    \begin{align}
        \tfrac{1}{2} - \tfrac{1}{k+1} \leq s < \tfrac{\beta}{2}.\label{Regime_1:reg_assumption}
    \end{align}
    Furthermore, we obtain polynomial growth bounds (\ref{Regime_1:poly_post_gron}, \ref{Regime_1:other_growth_bound}) for the $H^s$ norm of the solution $u$ if the corresponding initial condition $u_0\in H^s$.
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Clearly, \eqref{Regime_1:reg_assumption} satisfies both of the conditions stated in Remark \ref{Local:remark_verification}. Therefore, for $u_0 \in H^s$ there exists a unique corresonding local solution $u$ to \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq}. Given that \(s> \frac{1}{2} - \frac{1}{k+1}\), the Sobolev inequality guarantees that \(H^s \hookrightarrow L^{k+1}\). As such, Lemma \ref{Global:critical:general:Lk1_control} gives uniform control on the \(L^{k+1}\) norm of \(u\). Furthermore, we consider the mollified data $u_0^\varepsilon$ and $u^\varepsilon$, it's corresponding local evolution under \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq}.

    \underline{Case 1:} Assume that \(\frac{\beta-1}{2} <s < \frac{\beta}{2}\). Starting with Lemma \ref{global:critical:general:lem_energy} and Parseval's identity, we estimate
    \begin{align},
        \frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt} \left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|^2_{H^s_\beta} & \lesssim  \left|\left\langle \langle \partial_x\rangle_\beta^{2s-\beta}u^\varepsilon,\partial_x \left((u^\varepsilon)^k\right) \right\rangle_{L^2}\right|\notag                                                                                  \\ & = \left|\left\langle \langle\partial_x\rangle^\sigma_\beta u^\varepsilon, \langle \partial_x\rangle_\beta^{2s-\beta -\sigma}\partial_x\left((u^\varepsilon)^k\right)  \right\rangle_{L^2}\right|\notag                                                        \\
                                                             & \leq \left\|\langle \partial_x\rangle_\beta^\sigma u^\varepsilon\right\|_{L^{p}} \left\| \langle \partial_x \rangle_\beta^{2s - \beta - \sigma}\partial_x \left((u^\varepsilon) ^k\right)\right\|_{L^q}\notag
    \end{align}
    for some \(\sigma \in \mathbb{R}\) and \(1\leq p,q\leq \infty\) such that,
    \begin{equation}\label{Global:critical:general:first_holder_condition}
        1 = \tfrac{1}{p} + \tfrac{1}{q}.
    \end{equation}
    We now use Lemma \ref{Lem:multiplier_lemma} to obtain,
    \begin{align}
          \left\|\langle \partial_x\rangle_\beta^\sigma u^\varepsilon\right\|_{L^{p}} \left\| \langle \partial_x \rangle_\beta^{2s - \beta - \sigma}\partial_x \left((u^\varepsilon) ^k\right)\right\|_{L^q} \lesssim   \left\|\langle \partial_x\rangle^\sigma u^\varepsilon\right\|_{L^{p}} \left\| \langle \partial_x \rangle^{2s - \beta - \sigma+1} \left((u^\varepsilon) ^k\right)\right\|_{L^q}\label{Global:critical:general:case_1:first_holder}.
    \end{align}
    Focusing on the nonlinearity term in \eqref{Global:critical:general:case_1:first_holder} we assume that \(2s-\beta+1 - \sigma >0\) in order to use the fractional Leibniz rule like so,
    \begin{align}
        \left\| \langle \partial_x \rangle^{2s - \beta+1 - \sigma} \left((u^\varepsilon) ^k\right)\right\|_{L^q} & \lesssim \left\|(u^\varepsilon)^{k-1}\right\|_{L^{q_1}}\left\|\langle \partial_x\rangle^{2s-\beta+1-\sigma} u^\varepsilon\right\|_{L^{q_2}}\notag                                                   \\
                                                                                                   & = \left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|^{k-1}_{L^{q_1(k-1)}} \left\|\langle \partial_x\rangle^{2s-\beta+1-\sigma} u^\varepsilon\right\|_{L^{q_2}}\label{Global:critical:general:case_1:Fractional_liebniz}
    \end{align}
    giving us another H\"older integrability condition which, when combined with \eqref{Global:critical:general:first_holder_condition} gives us
    \begin{equation*}
        1 = \tfrac{1}{p} + \tfrac{1}{q_1}+ \tfrac{1}{q_2}.
    \end{equation*}
    Choosing these explicitly,
    \begin{align*}
        p & = k+1 & q_1 & = \tfrac{k+1}{k-1} & q_2 & = k+1.
    \end{align*}
    Now, combining \eqref{Global:critical:general:case_1:Fractional_liebniz} with \eqref{Global:critical:general:case_1:first_holder} we get,
    \begin{align}
        \frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|_{H^s_\beta} & \lesssim \left\|\langle \partial_x\rangle^\sigma u^\varepsilon\right\|_{L^{k+1}}\left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|^{k-1}_{L^{k+1}}\left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|_{W^{2s-\beta+1-\sigma, k+1}}\notag\\
        &= \left(\text{Term 1}\right)\cdot \left(\text{Term 2}\right)\cdot \left(\text{Term 3}\right)\label{Global:critical:case_1:terms_expansion}.
    \end{align}
    \begin{itemize}
        \item \underline{Term 1:} Using Corollary \ref{Interpolation:GNS:THM} for Term 1 we have that,
    \begin{align}
        \left\|\langle \partial_x\rangle^\sigma u^\varepsilon\right\|_{L^p} \lesssim \left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|_{H^s}^{\theta_1}\left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|_{L^{k+1}}^{1-\theta}\label{Global:critical:general:case_1:theta_1_ineq}.
    \end{align}
    where,
    \begin{align*}
        \theta_1 = \frac{\sigma}{s-\frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{k+1}}.
    \end{align*}
    Checking the validity of the interpolation we require,
     
  \par\vspace{\abovedisplayskip}\noindent
  \begin{minipage}{.48\textwidth}
    \def\maketag@@@# \theta_1 >0 \iff \sigma>0\label{Global:Regime_1:sigma_lb_1}{\hbox{\m@th\normalfont# \theta_1 >0 \iff \sigma>0\label{Global:Regime_1:sigma_lb_1}\,,}} 
    \begin{equation}
       \theta_1 >0 \iff \sigma>0\label{Global:Regime_1:sigma_lb_1}
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \hfill
  \begin{minipage}{.48\textwidth}
    \begin{equation}
       \theta_1 < 1 \iff \sigma < s-\tfrac{1}{2} + \tfrac{1}{k+1}\label{Global:Regime_1:sigma_ub_1}
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \par\vspace{\belowdisplayskip} 

    There is room for such a $\sigma$ to exist given in this case $s > \tfrac{\beta-1}{2}$.
    \item \underline{Term 2:} Here, we already have control from Lemma \ref{Global:critical:general:Lk1_control}. Using this, we yield,
    \begin{align}
        \left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|_{L^{k+1}}^{k-1} &\lesssim \mathscr{H}_{k,1,1}(u_0^\varepsilon)^{k-1}\label{Global:general:case_1:term_2_ineq}.
    \end{align}
    \item \underline{Term 3:} Interpolating Term 3 we obtain,
    \begin{align}
        \left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|_{W^{2s-\beta+1-\sigma,q_2}}\lesssim \left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|_{H^s}^{\theta_3}\left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|^{1-\theta_3}_{L^{k+1}}\label{Global:critical:general:case_1:theta_3_ineq}.
    \end{align}
    with
    \begin{align*}
        \theta_3 = \frac{2s-\beta+1-\sigma}{s-\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{k+1}}.
    \end{align*}
    Again, checking the validity of this interpolation, we require that,
     
  \par\vspace{\abovedisplayskip}\noindent
  \begin{minipage}{.48\textwidth}
    \def\maketag@@@# \theta_3 >0 \iff \sigma < 2s-\beta+1 \label{Regime_1:sigma_ub_2}{\hbox{\m@th\normalfont# \theta_3 >0 \iff \sigma < 2s-\beta+1 \label{Regime_1:sigma_ub_2}\,,}} 
    \begin{equation}
       \theta_3 >0 \iff \sigma < 2s-\beta+1 \label{Regime_1:sigma_ub_2}
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \hfill
  \begin{minipage}{.48\textwidth}
    \begin{equation}
      \theta_3 < 1 \iff \sigma > s-\beta + \tfrac{3}{2} - \tfrac{1}{k+1}\label{Regime_1:sigma_lb_2}
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \par\vspace{\belowdisplayskip} 

    Using these upper and lower bounds to check that there is room for $\sigma$ to exist,
    \begin{align*}
        s-\beta + \tfrac{3}{2} - \tfrac{1}{k+1} < 2s-\beta+1 \iff s > \tfrac{1}{2} - \tfrac{1}{k+1}
    \end{align*}
    which is satisfied by assumption \eqref{Regime_1:reg_assumption}.
    \end{itemize}
     We must now also check that our upper bound from Term 1 \eqref{Global:Regime_1:sigma_ub_1} agrees with the lower bound from Term 3 \eqref{Regime_1:sigma_lb_2} and vice versa with \eqref{Global:Regime_1:sigma_lb_1} and \eqref{Regime_1:sigma_ub_2}
    \begin{align*}
        s-\beta + \tfrac{3}{2} -\tfrac{1}{k+1} <s-\tfrac{1}{2} + \tfrac{1}{k+1} &\iff \beta >2-\tfrac{2}{k+1}\\
        0 < 2s-\beta+1 &\iff s > \tfrac{\beta-1}{2}
    \end{align*}
    both of which are satisfied by assumption. As such, we may take,
    \begin{align*}
        \max\left(0,s-\beta+\tfrac{3}{2} - \tfrac{1}{k+1}\right) < \sigma < \min\left(s-\tfrac{1}{2} + \tfrac{1}{k+1},2s-\beta+1\right).
    \end{align*}
    Returning to \eqref{Global:critical:case_1:terms_expansion} with \eqref{Global:critical:general:case_1:theta_1_ineq}, \eqref{Global:general:case_1:term_2_ineq} and \eqref{Global:critical:general:case_1:theta_3_ineq} we get that,
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|^2_{H^s_\beta} \lesssim \mathscr{H}_{k,1,1}(u_0)^{k-1} \left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|_{H^s}^{\theta_1 + \theta_3}\left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|_{L^{k+1}}^{(1-\theta_1)+(1-\theta_3)}
    \end{align*}
    where we have used \eqref{global:Hamiltonian_limit} to ensure the Hamiltonian constant is in terms of $u_0$. Using Lemma \ref{Global:critical:general:Lk1_control} on the remaining $L^{k+1}$ and invoking Lemma \ref{Lem:multiplier_lemma} to get a $H^s_\beta$ norm on the right hand side we obtain
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|^2_{H^s_\beta} \lesssim \mathscr{H}_{k,1,1}(u_0^\varepsilon)^{1-\alpha +k}\left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|_{H^s_\beta}^{\alpha}
    \end{align*}
    where $\alpha \coloneqq \theta_1+\theta_2$. In order to use Gr\"onwall's inequality on this expression we require that $\alpha \leq 2$ which is equivalent to our assumption that $\beta \geq 2-\frac{2}{k+1}$. Therefore, if $\alpha =2$, meaning $\beta = 2- \frac{2}{k+1}$, we have the exponential growth bound for any $t\in [0,T]$ after using Grownall's inequality, \eqref{global:Hamiltonian_limit}, \eqref{Global:solution_limit_Hs} to take $\varepsilon\to 0$ and finally Lemma \ref{Lem:multiplier_lemma} to change the $H^s_\beta$ norm to $H^s$,
    \begin{align}
        \left\|u(t)\right\|_{H^{s}} \leq \left\|u_0\right\|_{H^s_\beta}\exp\left(C(s,k,\beta,\mu)\mathscr{H}_{k,1,1}(u_0)^{k-1}t\right)\label{Regime_1:exponential_post_gron}
    \end{align}
    where $C(s,k,\beta,\mu)>0$. If $\alpha <2$ we get a polynomial growth bound after integrating the inequality and again using \eqref{global:Hamiltonian_limit}, \eqref{Global:solution_limit_Hs} and Lemma \ref{Lem:multiplier_lemma},
    \begin{align}
        \left\|u(t)\right\|_{H^s} \leq \left(\left\|u_0\right\|_{H^s_\beta}^{1-\alpha} + C(s,k,\beta,\mu) \mathscr{H}_{k,1,1}(u_0)^{k-1}(1-\alpha)t\right)^{\frac{2}{2-\alpha}}\label{Regime_1:poly_post_gron}.
    \end{align}
    Applying Lemma \ref{Lem:multiplier_lemma} to \eqref{Regime_1:exponential_post_gron} and \eqref{Regime_1:poly_post_gron} we obtain the stated growth bounds and thus the global result.
    
    \underline{Case 2:} Now assume that  \(\frac{1}{2} - \frac{1}{k+1} \leq s\leq \beta - \frac{3(k+1)-2}{2(k+1)}\).  Using Lemma \ref{global:critical:general:lem_energy} and Parseval's identity, we estimate,
    \begin{align}
        \frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt} \left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|^2_{H^s_\beta} &\leq \left|\left\langle \partial_x \langle\partial_x\rangle_\beta^{2s-\beta} u^\varepsilon, (u^\varepsilon)^k \right\rangle_{L^2}\right|\leq \left\|\partial_x\langle\partial_x\rangle_\beta^{2s-\beta}u^\varepsilon\right\|_{L^{k+1}} \left\|(u^\varepsilon)^k\right\|_{L^{\frac{k+1}{k}}} \notag         \\
        & \sim \left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|_{W^{2s-\beta+1,k+1}}\left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|^k_{L^{k+1}}\label{Global:critical:general:case_two:first_ineq}
    \end{align}
    where in the second line we used H\"older's inequality and in the third we used Lemma \ref{Lem:multiplier_lemma} to exchange the $\langle \partial_x\rangle_\beta$ multiplier for a $\langle \partial_x \rangle$ multiplier.
    Now, we will use the Sobolev inequality to verify that \(H^s(\mathbb{T})\hookrightarrow W^{2s-\beta+1,k+1}(\mathbb{T})\) i.e.,
    \begin{equation}\label{Global:critical:general:case_two:sobolev}
        \left\|u\right\|_{W^{2s-\beta+1,k+1}} \lesssim \left\|u\right\|_{H^s}.
    \end{equation}
    This gives us the following condition,
    \begin{align*}
        s-\tfrac{1}{2} \geq 2s - \beta +1 - \tfrac{1}{k+1}
    \end{align*}
    which is equivalent to our assumption that \(s\leq \beta +\frac{2-3(k+1)}{2(k+1)}\). As such, using \eqref{Global:critical:general:case_two:sobolev} in \eqref{Global:critical:general:case_two:first_ineq} we get that,
    \begin{align*}
        \frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt} \left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|^2_{H^s_\beta} \lesssim \left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|_{H^s} \left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|^k_{L^{k+1}}.
    \end{align*}
    From this, we use our uniform control of the $L^{k+1}$ norm from Lemma \ref{Global:critical:general:Lk1_control} and Lemma \ref{Lem:multiplier_lemma} to obtain,
    \begin{equation*}
        \frac{1}{2} \frac{d}{dt} \left\|u^\varepsilon\right\|^2_{H^s_\beta} \lesssim  \mathscr{H}_{k,1,1}(u_0^\varepsilon)^k\left\|u\right\|_{H^s_\beta}.
    \end{equation*}
    As such, integrating the inequality and using \eqref{global:Hamiltonian_limit} and \eqref{Global:solution_limit_Hs} to take $\varepsilon\to 0$, we yield the  growth bound,
    \begin{equation}\label{Regime_1:other_growth_bound}
        \left\|u(t)\right\|_{H^s_\beta} \leq \left(\left\|u_0\right\|_{H^s_\beta}^{\frac{1}{2}} +  C(s,k,\beta,\mu)\mathscr{H}_{k,1,1}(u_0)^k t\right)^2
    \end{equation}
    where $C(s,k,\beta,\mu)>0$. Once again, we use Lemma \ref{lem:multilinear_estimate} interchange the $H^s_\beta$ norm with the $H^s$ norm yielding the stated growth bound and thereby global well-posedness in the stated regime.

    Now, to tie our cases together and obtain global wellposedness and polynomial growth for \(\frac{1}{2} - \frac{1}{k+1} \leq s<\frac{\beta}{2}\) we demand that,
    \begin{align*}
        \tfrac{\beta-1}{2} \leq \beta -\tfrac{3(k+1)-2}{2(k+1)}
    \end{align*}
    which is indeed satisfied due to our \(\beta \geq 2-\frac{2}{k+1}\) assumption, completing the proof.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
We note that if $u(t)\in L^{k+1}$ for $t\in [0,T]$ where $T$ is the local existence time of $u$, the above proof forces no lower bound on $s$ other then the regularity needed to ensure local existence. This is because, the regularity condition in Theorem \ref{Regime_1_THM} that $s\geq \frac{1}{2} - \frac{1}{k+1}$ is simply to ensure that $H^s(\mathbb{T})\hookrightarrow L^{k+1}(\mathbb{T})$. If we begin with this assumption, we may weaken the regularity to the local condition. Although this result will not be directly used, it will be helpful to keep this in mind when we discuss the well-posedness of \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} in $L^{k+1}$ itself.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- lemma-9: body_md -->
\label{Frequency_cut:product_estimate}
    Let \(1<\beta\leq 3/2\) and $1\leq i\leq 3$. For \(w_1,w_2,w_3 \in H^{\beta/2}\) and \(v_1,v_2,v_3 \in H^s\), there is the multilinear estimate
    \begin{align*}
        \Big\|\phi_\beta(\partial_x) \prod_{j=1}^i w_j \prod_{l=i}^3 v_l\Big\|_{H^{\beta/2}}\lesssim \prod_{j=1}^i\left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}} \prod_{l=i}^3\left\|v\right\|_{H^s}
    \end{align*}
    if the following condition on \(s\) and $\beta$ is satisfied,
    \begin{align*}
        s \geq \tfrac{3-\beta}{4}.
    \end{align*}
<!-- end lemma-9 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
We prove the case when $v_1=v_2=v_3$ and $w_1=w_2 = w_3$, the general case follows in an identical fashion. We begin by noticing that \(\phi_\beta(\partial_x)\) smooths by \(\beta-1\) derivatives,
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|\phi_\beta(\partial_x)w^i v^{3-i}\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}\sim \left\|w^i v^{3-i}\right\|_{H^{1-\beta/2}}.
    \end{align*}
    Given that \(1-\beta/2>0\) we use the Fractional Libeniz rule to obtain,
    \begin{align}
        \left\|w^i v^{3-i}\right\|_{H^{1-\beta/2}} & \lesssim \left\|\langle \partial_x\rangle^{1-\beta/2} w^i\right\|_{L^{p_1}}\left\|v^{3-i}\right\|_{L^{q_1}} + \left\|w^{i}\right\|_{L^{\infty}}\left\|\langle \partial_x\rangle^{1-\beta/2}v^{3-i}\right\|_{L^2}\notag. \\
                                                       & = \text{Term }1 + \text{Term }2\label{Frequency_cut:Mulitilinear:Split}
    \end{align}
    where $p_1$ and $q_1$ satisfy the H\"older condition
    \begin{align}
         \tfrac{1}{2}=\tfrac{1}{p_1}+\tfrac{1}{q_1}\label{Global:frequency_cut:p_1_q_1_holder_cond}.
    \end{align}
    We inspect each of these terms separately and from them derive our necessary conditions on \(s\) and \(\beta\).
    \begin{itemize}
        \item \underline{Term 1:} Here, we use the fractional Leibniz rule again to obtain,
              \begin{align}
                  \left\|\langle \partial_x \rangle^{1-\beta/2}w^i\right\|_{L^{p_1}}\lesssim \left\|\langle\partial_x \rangle^{1-\beta/2}w\right\|_{L^{p_1}}\left\|w\right\|^{i-1}_{L^\infty}.\label{Frequency_cut_local:frac_lieb_key_step}
              \end{align}
              We demand the embedding \(H^{\beta/2}(\mathbb{T})\hookrightarrow W^{1-\beta/2,p_1}(\mathbb{T})\) which gives us the condition that,
              \begin{align}
                  \tfrac{\beta}{2} - \tfrac{1}{2} \geq 1-\tfrac{\beta}{2}-\tfrac{1}{p_1} \iff \tfrac{1}{p_1} \geq \tfrac{3}{2} - \beta. \label{Global:frequency_cut:multilinear:term1_p1_cond}
              \end{align}
              Here, \(p_1\) is well defined as $\beta>1$. When $i<3$, we also require that \(H^s(\mathbb{T}) \hookrightarrow L^{q_1(3-i)}(\mathbb{T})\), which yields the condition,
              \begin{align}
                  \tfrac{1}{(3-i)q_1} \geq \tfrac{1}{2}-s \iff \tfrac{1}{q_1} \geq \left(3-i\right)\left(\tfrac{1}{2} - s\right).\label{Global:Frequency_cut:multilinear:term1_Lq1_cond}
              \end{align}
              For $L^{q_1}$ to be a well-defined Lebesgue space, we require that
              \begin{align}
                   \left(3-i\right)\left(\tfrac{1}{2} - s\right)\leq 1 \iff s\geq 0 \label{Global:Frequency_cut:mutlilinear:term1_well_def_leb}
              \end{align}
              which is satisfied by assumption. Therefore,
              \begin{align*}
                  \left\|v\right\|^{3-i}_{L^{q_1}}\lesssim \left\|v\right\|^{3-i}_{H^s}.
              \end{align*}
              Combining conditions \eqref{Global:frequency_cut:multilinear:term1_p1_cond} and \eqref{Global:Frequency_cut:multilinear:term1_Lq1_cond} in our H\"older condition \eqref{Global:frequency_cut:p_1_q_1_holder_cond} we obtain,
              \begin{align}
                  \tfrac{1}{2} \geq \tfrac{3}{2}- \beta + (3-i)\left(\tfrac{1}{2}-s\right)  \iff  s \geq \tfrac{2-\beta}{2} \label{Frequency_cut:local:term_1:fin_cond}
              \end{align}
              and thus,
              \begin{align}
                  \text{Term }1 & \lesssim \left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}\left\|w\right\|^{i-1}_{L^\infty}\left\|v\right\|^{3-i}_{H^s}  \lesssim \left\|w\right\|^i_{H^{\beta/2}}\left\|v\right\|^{3-i}_{H^s}\label{Frequency_cut:local:term_1_ineq}
              \end{align}
              where the final inequality follows by the Sobolev embedding as $\beta>1$.
        \item \underline{Term 2:} We first use the Sobolev embedding to get that,
              \begin{align*}
                  \left\|w^i\right\|_{L^\infty} \lesssim \left\|w\right\|^i_{H^{\beta/2}}.
              \end{align*}
              If $i<3$, for the other norm in the product we use the Fractional Leibniz rule,
              \begin{align*}
                  \left\|\langle \partial_x\rangle^{1-\beta/2}v^{3-i}\right\|_{L^2} & \lesssim \left\|\langle \partial_x\rangle^{1-\beta/2} v \right\|_{L^{p_3}} \left\|v\right\|^{2-i}_{L^{q_3}}
              \end{align*}
              where we have the following H\"older condition,
              \begin{align}
                  \tfrac{1}{2} = \tfrac{1}{p_3} + \tfrac{2-i}{q_3}\label{Frequencycut:Multilinear:term_2:holder_cond}.
              \end{align}
              Now, we first require that \(H^s\hookrightarrow W^{1-\beta/2,p_3}\) which gives us the condition,
              \begin{align}
                  s-\tfrac{1}{2} = 1-\tfrac{\beta}{2} - \tfrac{1}{p_3} \iff \tfrac{1}{p_3} = \tfrac{3}{2} - \tfrac{\beta}{2} -s. \label{Frequencut:Multilinear:term_2:cond_1}
              \end{align}
              We know that $L^{p_3}$ is well defined as $\beta<3/2$. Now requiring that \(H^s \hookrightarrow L^{q_3}\) we yield,
              \begin{align}
                  \tfrac{1}{q_3} \geq \tfrac{1}{2} -s.\label{Frequencut:Multilinear:term_2:cond_2}
              \end{align}
              Combining conditions, \eqref{Frequencut:Multilinear:term_2:cond_1} and \eqref{Frequencut:Multilinear:term_2:cond_2} in \eqref{Frequencycut:Multilinear:term_2:holder_cond} we get that,
              \begin{align}
                  \tfrac{1}{2} \geq \tfrac{3}{2}-\tfrac{\beta}{2} - s+(2-i)(\tfrac{1}{2}-s) \iff s\geq \tfrac{3-\beta}{4}\label{Frequency_cut:local:term_2:fin_cond}
              \end{align}
              and resultingly,
              \begin{align}
                  \text{Term }2 \lesssim \left\|w\right\|^i_{H^{\beta/2}}\left\|v\right\|_{H^s}^{k-i}.\label{Frequency_cut:local:term2_ineq}
              \end{align}
    \end{itemize}
    Returning our findings \eqref{Frequency_cut:local:term_1_ineq} and  \eqref{Frequency_cut:local:term2_ineq} to \eqref{Frequency_cut:Mulitilinear:Split} we see that,
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|w^i v^{3-i}\right\|_{H^{1-\beta/2}} \lesssim \left\|w\right\|^i_{H^{\beta/2}}\left\|v\right\|^{3-i}_{H^s}
    \end{align*}
    which is what we wanted to show. We also note that our condition \eqref{Frequency_cut:local:term_2:fin_cond} is strictly stronger then our \eqref{Frequency_cut:local:term_1:fin_cond} and \eqref{Global:Frequency_cut:mutlilinear:term1_well_def_leb} conditions due to the \(1<\beta\leq 3/2\) assumption.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
\label{Regime_2:restriction_remark}
    If we consider arbitrary $k\geq 2$, the proof of Lemma \eqref{Frequency_cut:product_estimate} is easily adapted to obtain the stated product estimate for
    \begin{align*}
        s \geq \tfrac{k-\beta}{2(k-1)}.
    \end{align*}
    Since we must specialise to the $k=3$ case in our global argument however (see Remark \ref{Regime_2:restriction_rem2}) the estimate in this more general form serves no use.
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
Attempts to generalise Lemma \ref{Frequency_cut:product_estimate} to dimension 2 have been unsuccessful. The embedding of $H^{\beta/2} \hookrightarrow L^\infty$ fails in dimension 2 which is key to closing the estimate in the dimension 1 proof. If one instead attempts to avoid $L^\infty$ in \eqref{Frequency_cut_local:frac_lieb_key_step} by introducing exponents $p_3$ and $q_3$ such that,
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|\langle D_x \rangle^{1-\beta/2}w^i\right\|_{L^{p_1}}\lesssim \left\|\langle D_x \rangle^{1-\beta/2}w\right\|_{L^{p_3}}\left\|w\right\|^{i-1}_{L^{q_3}}
    \end{align*}
    the resulting embeddings in dimension 2 require,
    \begin{align*}
        s\geq 2- \tfrac{\beta-1}{2}.
    \end{align*}
    This leaves us above the low regularity threshold of $\beta/2$ meaning no progress can be made in the corresponding global argument.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- lemma-10: body_md -->
\label{thm:local_w_freq}
    Let $\mu \in \{-1,1\}$  and $1<\beta \leq 3/2$. \eqref{Global:Frequency_cut:general:PDE} is locally well-posed in $H^{\beta/2}$  if $v\in H^s$ such that,
    \begin{align*}
        s\geq \tfrac{3-\beta}{4}.
    \end{align*}
    Moreover, the corresponding solution $w_N(t)$ has existence time
    \begin{align*}
        T_{w_N \, \text{existence}} = \left\|w_{0,N}\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{2} \wedge T_{v_N \, \text{existence}}
    \end{align*}
    where $T_{v_N \, \text{existence}}$ is the existence time of $v_N(t)$ from Theorem \ref{Local_theory:lwp_main}.
<!-- end lemma-10 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
The local well-posedness of \eqref{Global:Frequency_cut:general:PDE} in \(H^{\beta/2}\) follows from our product estimate Lemma \ref{Frequency_cut:product_estimate}. Using Remark \ref{Local:remark_verification} to verify that this condition exceeds the local existence conditions of $v_N$. We note that in Regime 2 we are solely in the case where, $\beta < \tfrac{5}{3}$. Therefore, we require that,
\begin{align*}
     \tfrac{3-\beta}{4} \geq \tfrac{2-\beta}{2}
\end{align*}
which is satisfied by our assumptions on $\beta$.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
\label{Global:general:w_conservation}
    As seen in Lemma \ref{Global:critical:general:Lk1_control}, if we have initial data \(u_0\in L^{4}\) (i.e. \(u_0\in H^s\) for \(s\geq 1/4\)), we have uniform in time control of the \(L^{4}\) of the solution \(u\) to \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq}. Given that \(v_N\) is also a solution to
    \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} we have uniform control of it's \(L^{4}\) norm, provided that \(v_{0,N}\in L^{4}\). Furthermore, using that \(w_N = u-v_N\) and the triangle inequality,
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|w_N(t)\right\|_{L^{4}} = \left\|u(t)-v_N(t)\right\|_{L^{k+1}} &\leq \left\|u(t)\right\|_{L^{4}}+\left\|v_N(t)\right\|_{L^{4}}\\
         &\lesssim  \mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(u_0)\vee \mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(v_{0,N})\\
        &\sim \mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(u_0)
    \end{align*}
    where $t\in [0,T]$. Therefore, we obtain uniform control also  on the \(L^{4}\) norm of \(w_N\).
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\label{Regime_2:prop_result}
    Let $\mu\in \{-1,1\}$, $k=3$, $d=1$ and $5/4 < \beta \leq 3/2$. \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} is globally well-posed in $H^s(\mathbb{T})$ for,
    \begin{align*}
       \tfrac{9}{8} - \tfrac{\beta}{2} < s<\tfrac{\beta}{2}.
    \end{align*}
    Furthermore, we obtain a polynomial growth bound on the $H^s$ norm of our solution $u$.
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Fix \(T>0\) and take $N\gg 1$ such that,
    \begin{align*}
        \left\|v_{0,N}\right\|_{H^s} = \left\|\Pi_{\geq N}u_0\right\|_{H^s} \lesssim T^{-\frac{1}{2}}.
    \end{align*}
    Letting $T_{v_N \, \text{existence}}$ be the existence time of the local solution $v_N(t)\in H^s(\mathbb{T})$ to \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq} from Theorem \ref{Local_theory:lwp_main}, we recall
    that,
    \begin{align*}
        T_{v_N \, \text{existence}} \lesssim \left\|v_{0,N}\right\|_{H^s}^{-2}.
    \end{align*}
    Therefore, $T_{v_N \, \text{existence}} \lesssim T$, meaning there is a solution $v_N(t)$ on $[0,T]$ such that,
    \begin{align}
       \sup_{t\in [0,T]}\left\|v_N(t)\right\|_{H^s} \lesssim T^{-\frac{1}{2}}.\label{Global:frequency_cut:v_T_bound}
    \end{align}
    Now that we have fixed $N$, we denote $w_N$ and $v_N$ as $w$ and $v$ respectively. Let $w$ be the local solution from Lemma \ref{thm:local_w_freq} with existence time $T_{w \text{existence}}$ where $v\in H^s$ such that, $ s \geq \tfrac{3-\beta}{4}$ which is satisfied by assumption. We now show that the solution $w$ of \eqref{Global:Frequency_cut:general:PDE} can be extended to time \(T\). After doing this, we may combine our solutions \(v\) and \(w\) to form the global solution \(u =v+w\) to \eqref{Gen-BOBBM-eq}. Before we begin, it is important to note that we require $s\geq 1/4$ in order to ensure the embedding \(H^s(\mathbb{T})\hookrightarrow L^{4}(\mathbb{T})\) by the Sobolev inequality. Given $\beta >1$, this is also satisfied by assumption. This means we have uniform in time control over the \(L^{4}\) norm of \(v\) by our Hamiltonian conservation \eqref{Conservation:Hamiltonian} and also uniform in time control over the \(L^{4}\) norm of \(w\) by Remark \ref{Global:general:w_conservation}.

    Multiplying \eqref{Global:Frequency_cut:general:PDE} by the solution \(w\), integrating over \(\mathbb{T}\) and integrating by parts we get,
    \begin{align*}
        \int_\mathbb{T} w \partial_t\left(1+|\partial_x|^\beta\right) w \, dx = \frac{1}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{3} \binom{3}{i} \int_{\mathbb{T}} w^{i}v^{3-i}\partial_x w\, dx.
    \end{align*}
    After an application of Parseval's identity on the left hand side we see that,
    \begin{align}
        \frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|w\right\|^2_{H^{\beta/2}}\lesssim \frac{1}{3}\sum_{i=1}^{3} \binom{3}{i}\int_{\mathbb{T}}w^i v^{3-i}\partial_x w \, dx.\notag
    \end{align}
    We first note that the $i=3$ term vanishes by an integration by parts. Furthermore, in the sense of Fourier multipliers, \(\partial_x = - \mathcal{H} |\partial_x|\) where $\mathcal{H}$ is the Hilbert transform. Using this, we split the derivative in the $i=1,2$ case in the following way,
    \begin{align*}
        \int_{\mathbb{T}}w^i v^{3-i}\partial_x w \, dx & = -\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(|\partial_x|^{1-\beta/2} w^i v^{3-i}\right)\mathcal{H}|\partial_x|^{\beta/2}w \, dx.
    \end{align*}
    Therefore we have,
    \begin{align}
        \frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|w\right\|^2_{H^{\beta/2}} \lesssim \sum_{i=1}^2\left|\int_{\mathbb{T}}\left(|\partial_x|^{1-\beta/2} w^i v^{3-i}\right)\mathcal{H}|\partial_x|^{\beta/2}w \, dx\right|.\label{Global:lower:master_lesssim}
    \end{align}
    We set out to bound the right hand side integral by a power of the \(H^{\beta/2}\) norm of \(w\) less than $2$ up to a constant that is uniform in time. Fixing $i\in \{1,2\}$,
    \begin{align}
        \left|\int_{\mathbb{T}} \left(|\partial_x|^{1-\beta/2} w^i v^{3-i}\right)\mathcal{H} |\partial_x|^{\beta/2} w\, dx\right| & \lesssim \left\|w^i v^{3-i}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{1-\beta/2}} \left\|w\right\|_{\dot{H}^{\beta/2}} \lesssim \left\|w^i v^{3-i}\right\|_{H^{1-\beta/2}} \left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}\label{Global:First_split}.
    \end{align}
    With a single power of our target space norm we now need to estimate the nonlinear term. To do so, we first use the fractional Leibniz rule (noting that \(1-\beta/2 >0\)) to obtain,
    \begin{align}
        \left\|w^i v^{3-i}\right\|_{H^{1-\beta/2}} & \lesssim \left\|w^i\right\|_{W^{1-\beta/2,p_1}}\left\|v^{3-i}\right\|_{L^{q_1}} + \left\|w^i\right\|_{L^{p_2}}\left\|v^{3-i}\right\|_{W^{1-\beta/2, q_2}}\notag \\
                                                       & = \text{Term 1} + \text{Term 2}\label{Global:term_A_term_B_split}
    \end{align}
    where we have the H\"older condition,
    \begin{align}
        \tfrac{1}{2} = \tfrac{1}{p_1} + \tfrac{1}{q_1} = \tfrac{1}{p_2} + \tfrac{1}{q_2}\label{Global:low:first_hold_cond}.
    \end{align}
    We will now treat these two terms separately,
    \begin{itemize}
        \item \underline{Term 1:} For this term we make further distinction,
              \begin{align*}
                  \text{Term 1} = \left\|w^i\right\|_{W^{1-\beta/2,p_1}}\left\|v^{3-i}\right\|_{L^{q_1}} = (\text{Term 1.1})\cdot(\text{Term 1.2}).
              \end{align*}
              \begin{itemize}
                  \item \underline{Term 1.1:} If \(i=1\), we interpolate to obtain,
                        \begin{align*}
                            \left\|w\right\|_{W^{1-\beta/2,p_1}} \lesssim \left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{\theta^*_1}\left\|w\right\|_{L^{4}}^{1-\theta^*_1}
                        \end{align*}
                        where,
                        \begin{align*}
                            \theta^*_1 = \frac{1-\frac{\beta}{2} + \frac{1}{4} - \frac{1}{p_1}}{\frac{\beta}{2} - \frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{4}}.
                        \end{align*}
                        Inorder for the interpolation to work we require that \(0<\theta^*_1<1\),
                         
  \par\vspace{\abovedisplayskip}\noindent
  \begin{minipage}{.40\textwidth}
    \def\maketag@@@#.40{\hbox{\m@th\normalfont#.40,}} 
    \begin{equation}
      \theta^*_1 >0 \iff \tfrac{1}{p_1}<\tfrac{5}{4}-\tfrac{\beta}{2}\label{Global:low:special_term_1.1_p_1_upper}
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \hfill
  \begin{minipage}{.50\textwidth}
    \begin{equation}
      \theta^*_1 < 1 \iff \tfrac{1}{p_1}> \tfrac{3}{2}-\beta\label{Global:low:special_term_1.1_p_1_lower}
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \par\vspace{\belowdisplayskip} 

                        Therefore, for there to exist such a \(1/p_1\) we require that there is space between our upper and lower bounds which is satisfied by our $\beta>1$ assumption. Moving on to the \(i=2\) case, we encounter yet more terms after an application of the fractional Leibniz rule to Term 1.1,
                        \begin{align*}
                            \left\|w^2\right\|_{W^{1-\beta/2,p_1}} \lesssim  \left\|w\right\|_{W^{1-\beta/2,p_3}}\left\|w\right\|_{L^{q_3}}= (\text{Term 1.1.1})\cdot (\text{Term 1.1.2})
                        \end{align*}
                        where we have the H\"older condition,
                        \begin{align}
                            \tfrac{1}{p_1} = \tfrac{1}{p_3} + \tfrac{1}{q_3}.\label{Global:low:term1.1_holder}
                        \end{align}
                        \begin{itemize}
                            \item \underline{Term 1.1.1:} Here, we encounter an interpolation identical to our \(i=1\) case for Term 1.1 except with \(p_3\) instead of \(p_1\). That is, we have,
                                  \begin{align*}
                                      \left\|w\right\|_{W^{1-\beta/2,p_3}} \lesssim \left\|w\right\|^{\theta_1}_{H^{\beta/2}}\left\|w\right\|^{1-\theta_1}_{L^{k+1}}
                                  \end{align*}
                                  where,
                                  \begin{align*}
                                      \theta_1 = \frac{1-\frac{\beta}{2} + \frac{1}{4} - \frac{1}{p_3}}{\frac{\beta}{2} - \frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{4}}.
                                  \end{align*}
                                  Furthermore, by \eqref{Global:low:special_term_1.1_p_1_lower} we get that,
                                   
  \par\vspace{\abovedisplayskip}\noindent
  \begin{minipage}{0.5\textwidth}
    \def\maketag@@@#0.5{\hbox{\m@th\normalfont#0.5,}} 
    \begin{equation}
       \tfrac{1}{p_3} < \tfrac{5}{4} - \tfrac{\beta}{2}\label{Global:low:term1.1.1_p_3_upper}
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \hfill
  \begin{minipage}{0.4\textwidth}
    \begin{equation}
      \tfrac{1}{p_3} > \tfrac{3}{2}-\beta\label{Global:low:term1.1.1_p_3}
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \par\vspace{\belowdisplayskip} 

                            \item \underline{ Term 1.1.2:} Interpolating we yield,
                                  \begin{align*}
                                      \left\|w\right\|_{L^{q_3}}\lesssim \left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{\theta_2}\left\|w\right\|_{L^{k+1}}^{(1-\theta_2)}
                                  \end{align*}
                                  where,
                                  \begin{align*}
                                      \theta_2 = \frac{\frac{1}{4} - \frac{1}{q_3}}{\frac{\beta}{2} - \frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{4}}.
                                  \end{align*}
                                  Checking the validity of our interpolation,
                                   
  \par\vspace{\abovedisplayskip}\noindent
  \begin{minipage}{0.45\textwidth}
    \def\maketag@@@#0.45{\hbox{\m@th\normalfont#0.45,}} 
    \begin{equation}
      \theta_2>0 \iff \tfrac{1}{q_3} <\tfrac{1}{4}\label{Global:low:term1.1.2_q_3_upper}
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \hfill
  \begin{minipage}{0.45\textwidth}
    \begin{equation}
      \theta_2 <1 \iff \tfrac{1}{q_3} > 0\label{Global:low:term1.1.2_q_3_lower}.
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \par\vspace{\belowdisplayskip} 

                                  Clearly there is room for such a \(q_3\) to exist.
                        \end{itemize}
                        Combining our conditions \eqref{Global:low:term1.1.1_p_3} and \eqref{Global:low:term1.1.2_q_3_lower} in \eqref{Global:low:term1.1_holder} we obtain,
                        \begin{align*}
                            \tfrac{1}{p_1} > \tfrac{3}{2}-\beta
                        \end{align*}
                        and doing the same for \eqref{Global:low:term1.1.1_p_3_upper} and \eqref{Global:low:term1.1.2_q_3_upper},
                        \begin{align}
                            \tfrac{1}{p_1} < \tfrac{3}{2} -\tfrac{\beta}{2}\label{Global:lower:p_1upper}.
                        \end{align}
                        Verifying that there is enough room,
                        \begin{align*}
                            \tfrac{3}{2}-\beta < \tfrac{3}{2} -\tfrac{\beta}{2} \iff \tfrac{\beta}{2} > 0
                        \end{align*}
                        which is satisfied given \(\beta>1\).
                  \item \underline{Term 1.2:} Interpolating \(v\) between \(H^s\) and \(L^{4}\),
                        \begin{align*}
                            \left\|v^{3-i}\right\|_{L^{q_1}} = \left\|v\right\|^{3-i}_{L^{(3-i)q_1}} \lesssim \left\|v\right\|^{(3-i)\theta_3}_{H^s}\left\|v\right\|^{(3-i)(1-\theta_3)}_{L^{4}}
                        \end{align*}
                        where
                        \begin{align*}
                            \theta_3 = \frac{\frac{1}{4} - \frac{1}{q_1(3-i)}}{s-\frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{4+1}}.
                        \end{align*}
                        Checking the interpolation's validity,
                         
  \par\vspace{\abovedisplayskip}\noindent
  \begin{minipage}{0.3\textwidth}
    \def\maketag@@@#0.3{\hbox{\m@th\normalfont#0.3,}} 
    \begin{equation}
       \theta_3 > 0 \iff \tfrac{1}{q_1} < \tfrac{3-i}{4}, \notag 
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \hfill
  \begin{minipage}{0.6\textwidth}
    \begin{equation}
      \theta_3<1 \iff \tfrac{1}{q_1} > (3-i)\left(\tfrac{1}{2} - s\right).\label{Global:low:term1.2:q_1_condition}
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \par\vspace{\belowdisplayskip} 

                        Checking if there exists such a \(q_1\),
                        \begin{align*}
                            (3-i)\left(\tfrac{1}{2} - s\right) < \tfrac{3-i}{4} \iff s> \tfrac{1}{4}
                        \end{align*}
                        which is satisfied by assumption. Given that \(3-i\) is maximised for \(i=1\), we can write \eqref{Global:low:term1.2:q_1_condition} as,
                        \begin{align}
                            \tfrac{1}{q_1} > 2\left(\tfrac{1}{2} - s\right)\vee 0\label{Global:low:term1.2:q_1_lower_bound}.
                        \end{align}
              \end{itemize}
              Now, combining our \eqref{Global:low:special_term_1.1_p_1_lower} and \eqref{Global:low:term1.2:q_1_lower_bound} conditions in our H\"older condition \eqref{Global:low:first_hold_cond} we obtain,
              \begin{align*}
                  \tfrac{1}{2} > \tfrac{3}{2}-\beta + 2\left(\tfrac{1}{2} -s\right)\vee 0.
              \end{align*}
              For \(s>1/2\),
              \begin{align*}
                  \tfrac{1}{2} > \tfrac{3}{2}-\beta \iff \beta> 1
              \end{align*}
              which is satisfied by assumption. For \(s<1/2\),
              \begin{align*}
                  \tfrac{1}{2} > \tfrac{3}{2} - \beta + 2\left(\tfrac{1}{2} -s\right) \iff s> 1 - \tfrac{\beta}{2}
              \end{align*}
              which is satisfied by our assumption that $s>9/8-\beta/2$. Now, writing our expanded form of Term 1 we see that for \(i=1\),
              \begin{align}
                  (\text{Term 1})\cdot\left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}  &\lesssim \left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{\theta_1^*+1}\left\|w\right\|^{(1-\theta^*_1)}_{L^{4}}\left\|v\right\|^{2\theta_3}_{H^s}\left\|v\right\|^{2(1-\theta_3)}_{L^{4}}\notag \\
                                                                         & \lesssim T^{-\theta_3}\mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(u_0)^{(1-\theta_1^*)} \mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(v_0)^{2(1-\theta_3)}\left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{\theta_1^* +1}\label{Global:lower:expansion_term1_one}
              \end{align}
              where we have used Remark \ref{Global:general:w_conservation}, Lemma \ref{Global:critical:general:Lk1_control}, and \eqref{Global:frequency_cut:v_T_bound} to control the supremum of the $H^s$ norm of $v$. Doing the same for \(i=2\),
              \begin{align}
                  (\text{Term 1})\cdot\left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}  &\lesssim \left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{\theta_1 + (i-1)\theta_2 + 1}\left\|w\right\|_{L^{4}}^{(1-\theta_1) + (i-1)(1-\theta_2)}\left\|v\right\|_{H^s}^{(3-i)\theta_3}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{(3-i)(1-\theta_3)}\notag \\
                                                                         & \lesssim  T^{-\frac{(3-i)\theta_3}{2}}\mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(u_0)^{1-\theta_1 + (i-1)(1-\theta_2)}\notag\\
                                                                         &\hphantom{XXXXXXXXXXX}\times \mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(v_0)^{(3-i)(1-\theta_3)} \left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{\theta_1 + (i-1)\theta_2 +1}\label{Global:lower:expansion_term1_i}.
              \end{align}

        \item \underline{Term 2:} We now turn our attention to Term 2 from \eqref{Global:term_A_term_B_split} where we estimate the two norms in the product seperately,
              \begin{align*}
                  \text{Term 2} =\left\|w^i\right\|_{L^{p_2}}\left\|v^{3-i}\right\|_{W^{1-\beta/2,q_2}} \eqqcolon (\text{Term 2.1})\cdot(\text{Term 2.2}).
              \end{align*}
              \begin{itemize}
                  \item \underline{Term 2.1:} Interpolating we get,
                        \begin{align*}
                            \left\|w^i\right\|_{L^{p_2}} = \left\|w\right\|^i_{L^{ip_2}} \lesssim \left\|w\right\|^{i\theta_4}_{H^{\beta/2}}\left\|w\right\|^{i(1-\theta_4)}_{L^{4}}
                        \end{align*}
                        where,
                        \begin{align*}
                            \theta_4 & = \frac{\frac{1}{4} - \frac{1}{ip_2}}{\frac{\beta}{2} -\frac{1}{2}+\frac{1}{4}}.
                        \end{align*}
                        Verifying the interpolation's validity,
                         
  \par\vspace{\abovedisplayskip}\noindent
  \begin{minipage}{0.45\textwidth}
    \def\maketag@@@#0.45{\hbox{\m@th\normalfont#0.45,}} 
    \begin{equation}
       \theta_4>0 \iff \tfrac{1}{p_2} < \tfrac{i}{4}\label{Global:lower:Term2.1:p_2_upper}
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \hfill
  \begin{minipage}{0.45\textwidth}
    \begin{equation}
      \theta_4<1 \iff \tfrac{1}{p_2} > 0.\label{Global:p_2_lower:Regime_2}
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \par\vspace{\belowdisplayskip} 

                        There is clearly room for there to exist such a \(p_2\).
                  \item \underline{Term 2.2:} For the \(i=2\) case we interpolate to get,
                        \begin{align*}
                            \left\|v\right\|_{W^{1-\beta/2,q_2}} \lesssim \left\|v\right\|^{\theta_5^*}_{H^s}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{1-\theta_5^*}
                        \end{align*}
                        where,
                        \begin{align*}
                            \theta_5^* = \frac{1-\frac{\beta}{2} + \frac{1}{4} - \frac{1}{q_2}}{s-\frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{4}}.
                        \end{align*}
                        Requiring validity,
                         
  \par\vspace{\abovedisplayskip}\noindent
  \begin{minipage}{0.45\textwidth}
    \def\maketag@@@#0.45{\hbox{\m@th\normalfont#0.45,}} 
    \begin{equation}
       \theta_5^* >0 \iff \tfrac{1}{q_2} < 1-\tfrac{\beta}{2} + \tfrac{1}{4},\notag
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \hfill
  \begin{minipage}{0.45\textwidth}
    \begin{equation}
       \theta_5^* < 1 \iff \tfrac{1}{q_2}>\tfrac{3}{2} - \tfrac{\beta}{2}-s.\notag
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \par\vspace{\belowdisplayskip} 

                        Checking that such a \(q_2\) can exist,
                        \begin{align*}
                            \tfrac{3}{2} -\tfrac{\beta}{2} -s < 1-\tfrac{\beta}{2} + \tfrac{1}{4} \iff s> \tfrac{1}{4}
                        \end{align*}
                        which is satisfied by assumption. If \(i=1\) we distinguish further via another application of the fractional Leibniz rule,
                        \begin{align*}
                            \left\|v^{2}\right\|_{W^{1-\beta/2, q_2}} & \lesssim \left\|v\right\|_{W^{1-\beta/2,p_4}}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{q_4}} \\
                                                                            & = (\text{Term 2.2.1})\cdot (\text{Term 2.2.2}).\end{align*}
where we have the H\"older condition,
\begin{align}
    \tfrac{1}{q_2} = \tfrac{1}{p_4} + \tfrac{1}{q_4}\label{Regime_2:q_2:holder_con}
\end{align}
                        \begin{itemize}
                            \item \underline{Term 2.2.1:}  Interpolating much as we did for Term 2.2 in the \(i=2\) case,
                                  \begin{align*}
                                      \left\|v\right\|_{W^{1-\beta/2, p_4}}\lesssim \left\|v\right\|_{H^s}^{\theta_5} \left\|v\right\|_{L^{k+1}}^{1-\theta_5}
                                  \end{align*}
                                  where,
                                  \begin{align*}
                                      \theta_5 = \frac{1-\frac{\beta}{2} + \frac{1}{4} - \frac{1}{p_4}}{s-\frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{4}}
                                  \end{align*}
                                  where validity follows identically from the \(i=2\) case from Term 2.2. Note that we  have the condition,
                                  \begin{align}
                                      \tfrac{1}{p_4}>\tfrac{3}{2} - \tfrac{\beta}{2}-s.\label{Global:low:term2.2.1:p_4_lower}
                                  \end{align}
                            \item \underline{Term 2.2.2:} Again interpolating we yield,
                                  \begin{align*}
                                      \left\|v\right\|_{L^{q_4}} \lesssim \left\|v\right\|_{H^s}^{\theta_6}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{(1-\theta_6)}
                                  \end{align*}
                                  where,
                                  \begin{align*}
                                      \theta_6 = \frac{\frac{1}{4}-\frac{1}{q_4}}{s-\frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{4}}.
                                  \end{align*}
                                  Checking for validity,
                                   
  \par\vspace{\abovedisplayskip}\noindent
  \begin{minipage}{0.3\textwidth}
    \def\maketag@@@#0.3{\hbox{\m@th\normalfont#0.3,}} 
    \begin{equation}
      \theta_6>0 \iff \tfrac{1}{q_4} < \tfrac{1}{4},\notag
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \hfill
  \begin{minipage}{0.6\textwidth}
    \begin{equation}
      \theta_6 <1\iff \tfrac{1}{q_4} > \tfrac{1}{2}-s\label{Global:low:q_4_lower}
    \end{equation}
  \end{minipage} 
  \par\vspace{\belowdisplayskip} 

                                  Checking that there is space for such a \(q_4\) to exist,
                                  \begin{align*}
                                      \tfrac{1}{2}-s<\tfrac{1}{4}\iff s> \tfrac{1}{4}.
                                  \end{align*}
                                  which is satisfied by assumption.
                        \end{itemize}
                        Using \eqref{Global:low:term2.2.1:p_4_lower} and \eqref{Global:low:q_4_lower} in our H\"older condition \eqref{Regime_2:q_2:holder_con} we get that,
                        \begin{align*}
                            \tfrac{1}{q_2} > \tfrac{3}{2}-\tfrac{\beta}{2}-s + (\tfrac{1}{2} -s) \vee 0.
                        \end{align*}
                        Now using this and \eqref{Global:p_2_lower:Regime_2} in our H\"older condition \eqref{Global:low:first_hold_cond} we obtain,
                        \begin{align*}
                            \tfrac{1}{2} > \tfrac{3}{2}-\tfrac{\beta}{2}-s + (2-i)(\tfrac{1}{2} -s)\vee 0 \iff s > \tfrac{3-\beta}{4}
                        \end{align*}
                        which follows from the assumption that our local result is satisfied.
              \end{itemize}
              Now we can write our expanded estimate for Term 2, for $i=2$,
              \begin{align}
                  \left(\text{Term 2}\right)\cdot \left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}} & \lesssim \left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{2\theta_4+1}\left\|w\right\|^{2(1-\theta_4)}_{L^{4}}\left\|v\right\|_{H^s}^{\theta_5^*}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{1-\theta_5^*}\notag \\
                                                                                     & \lesssim  T^{-\frac{\theta_5^*}{2}}\mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(u_0)^{2(1-\theta_4)}\mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(v_0)^{1-\theta_5^*}\left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{(2)\theta_4 +1}.\label{Global:lower:expansion_term2_one}
              \end{align}
              For \(i=1\),
              \begin{align}
                  (\text{Term 2})\cdot \left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}} & \lesssim \left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{\theta_4 + 1}\left\|w\right\|_{L^{4}}^{1-\theta_4}\left\|v\right\|_{H^s}^{\theta_5 + \theta_6}\left\|v\right\|_{L^{4}}^{\theta_5 + \theta_6}\notag \\
                                                                          & \lesssim T^{-\frac{\theta_5 + \theta_6}{2}}\mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(u_0)^{1-\theta_4} \mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(v_0)^{\theta_5 + \theta_6}\left\|w\right\|^{\theta_4 +1}_{H^{\beta/2}}\label{Global:lower:expansion_term2_i}
              \end{align}
              where again we have used Remark \eqref{Global:general:w_conservation}, Lemma \ref{Global:critical:general:Lk1_control} and \eqref{Global:frequency_cut:v_T_bound}.

    \end{itemize}
    Returning to \eqref{Global:First_split} with \eqref{Global:lower:expansion_term1_one}, \eqref{Global:lower:expansion_term1_i}, \eqref{Global:lower:expansion_term2_one} and \eqref{Global:lower:expansion_term2_i} we get that,
    \begin{align*}
        &\int_\mathbb{T} \left|\left(|\partial_x|^{1-\beta/2} w^i v^{3-i}\right)\mathcal{H} |\partial_x|^{\beta/2} w\right|\, dx
         \lesssim \begin{cases}
          C^{\text{Term 1}}_{1}\left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{\theta_1^* +1} + C^{\text{Term 2}}_{1}\left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{\theta_4 +1}                & i=1  \\
          C^{\text{Term 1}}_{i}\left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{\theta_1 + (i-1)\theta_2 + 1} + C^{\text{Term 1}}_{i}\left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{i\theta_4+1} & i=2
                                                               \end{cases}
    \end{align*}
    where,
    \begin{align*}
        &C_i^{\text{Term 1}} \coloneqq C_i^{\text{Term 1}}(s,\beta,u_0,v_0)\\
          &\hphantom{X}= \begin{cases}
            T^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(u_0)^{(1-\theta_1^*)}\mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(v_0)^{2(1-\theta_3)}, & i=1\\
            T^{-\theta_3}\mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(u_0)^{1-\theta_1 + (i-1)(1-\theta_2)}\mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(v_0)^{(3-i)(1-\theta_3)} & i=2
        \end{cases}\\\\
        &C_i^{\text{Term 2}} \coloneqq C_i^{\text{Term 2}}(s,\beta,u_0,v_0)\\
        &\hphantom{X}=\begin{cases}
            T^{-\frac{\theta_5 + \theta_6}{2}}\mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(u_0)^{i(1-\theta_4)}\mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(v_0)^{\theta_5 + \theta_6}, & i=1\\
            T^{-\frac{\theta_5^*}{2}}\mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(u_0)^{2(1-\theta_4)}\mathscr{H}_{3,1,1}(v_0)^{1-\theta_5^*}, & i=2
        \end{cases}
    \end{align*}
    Using this in \eqref{Global:lower:master_lesssim} we obtain,
      \begin{align}
        \frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dt}\left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^2 &\lesssim C_1^{\text{Term 1}}\left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{\theta_1^* +1} + C_1^{\text{Term 2}}\left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{\theta_4 +1}\notag\\
         &\hphantom{X}+ C_{2}^{\text{Term 1}}\left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{\theta_1+ \theta_2+1} + C_2^{\text{Term 2}}\left\|w\right\|_{H^{\beta/2}}^{2\theta_4 + 1}\label{Global:freq_cut:cubic_pregron}
    \end{align}
     In order for this inequality to be amenable to integration, we require from the first term that \(\theta_1^* +1 < 2\) which we verified by checking \(\theta_1^* < 1\). Next, we require that \(i\theta_4 +1 <2\). That is, we need
      \begin{align*}
        \frac{\frac{i}{4} - \frac{1}{p_2}}{\frac{\beta}{2} - \frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{4}} < 1 \iff \frac{1}{p_2} > \frac{1+i}{4} - \frac{\beta}{2}.
    \end{align*}
    To ensure we have enough space for \(p_2\) to exist we must return to \eqref{Global:lower:Term2.1:p_2_upper}. We require that,
    \begin{align*}
         \tfrac{1+i}{4} - \tfrac{\beta}{2} < \tfrac{i}{4}
        \iff \beta > \tfrac{1}{2}
    \end{align*}
    which is satisfied by our \(\beta > 1\) assumption.
    Lastly, we need that \(\theta_1 + \theta_2+1<2\),
    \begin{align}
        \theta_1 + \theta_2 + 1\leq 2 & \iff \frac{1-\frac{\beta}{2} + \frac{1}{4} - \frac{1}{p_3}}{\frac{\beta}{2} - \frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{4}} + \frac{\frac{1}{4} - \frac{1}{q_3}}{\frac{\beta}{2} - \frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{4}} < 1 \notag  \\
                                           & \iff \frac{1-\frac{\beta}{2} + \frac{1}{2} - \frac{1}{p_1}}{\frac{\beta}{2} -\frac{1}{2} + \frac{1}{4}} < 1        \notag \\
                                           & \iff \tfrac{1}{p_1} > \tfrac{7}{4} - \beta \label{Global:low:p_1_gronwall_condnow}
    \end{align}
    where in the second line we used \eqref{Global:low:term1.1_holder}. Given that $5/4<\beta < 3/2$ it is clear that $p_1$ is well defined. Furthermore, we need to check that there is space for \(p_1\) to exist within our existing upper bounds. Note that of \eqref{Global:lower:p_1upper} and \eqref{Global:low:special_term_1.1_p_1_upper}, \eqref{Global:low:special_term_1.1_p_1_upper} is the stricter condition. Therefore, we require that,
    \begin{align*}
        \tfrac{7}{4} - \beta < \tfrac{5}{4} - \tfrac{\beta}{2} \iff \beta>1 
    \end{align*}
    which is satisfied by assumption.
